I have successfully implemented the core foundations for the "Voluntary State" and the "Voluntary Tax" system. Here is a summary of the progress:

1.  **Persistent Treasury Infrastructure**: Added `matching_pools` and `treasury_transactions` tables to the SQLite store.
2.  **Upgraded Treasury Core**: The `TreasuryManager` now supports multi-token pools and persistent transaction logging for all deposits and matching allocations.
3.  **Automated Quadratic Matching**: The `CrowdfundingEngine` now automatically allocates matching funds from the treasury when a proposal meets its funding goal, ensuring the state only "spends" what its citizens have voluntarily contributed.
4.  **Treasury API**: Implemented REST endpoints for balance tracking, transaction history, and voluntary deposits.
5.  **Integrated Frontend Dashboard**: Created a new "Treasury" tab in the UI allowing citizens to audit matching pools in real-time and make voluntary tax contributions.

- **Direct-to-subject voluntary tax routing**: Allowing citizens to target their "tax" to specific committees (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) instead of a general pool.
- **Reputation-weighted matching pool rewards**: Citizens with high reputation could receive small rewards or higher matching weights.

[PROJECT_MEMORY]

# LiquidGov: Project Architecture & Architectural Decisions

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is structured as a TypeScript-native monorepo that integrates a robust backend API, a reactive frontend, and a unique **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol (APEP)**. The architecture is designed to minimize "governance overhead" by automating as much of the project lifecycle as possible.

- **Backend**: Node.js/Express-based REST API with real-time capabilities via Socket.io.
- **Frontend**: Vite + React + Tailwind CSS v4, optimized for both desktop and mobile (PWA).
- **Database**: SQLite via `better-sqlite3`, providing high-performance, persistent storage for governance state without the complexity of a separate database server.

## 2. The Autonomous Engine (APEP)
The most distinctive pattern in this project is the **Executive Protocol** (`src/core/protocol/` and `src/core/repository.ts`). This system treats the repository itself as a managed asset:
- **Dual-Direction Intelligent Merge**: Automatically forward-merges feature branches into `main` and reverse-merges `main` back to prevent drift.
- **Documentation Governance**: Mandatory files like `ROADMAP.md` and `TODO.md` are not just static docs; they are dynamically synchronized via codebase scanning (regex/grep) during every protocol cycle.
- **Continuous Execution**: The `AutonomousWatchdog` ensures the protocol runs on a loop, making the repository "self-healing" and "self-documenting."

## 3. Governance Patterns: The "Voluntary State"
The project implements a circular governance economy based on the following decisions:
- **Liquid Power & Delegation**: Unlike simple 1-token-1-vote systems, power is subject-specific. A user can be an expert in "Infrastructure" but a novice in "Education." Power flows through a directed delegation graph.
- **Quadratic Everything**:
    - **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates the "tyranny of the majority" by making each additional vote for a single proposal exponentially more expensive.
    - **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a larger number of individual contributors rather than just large whales.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A deliberate decision was made to reward voluntary financial support with subject-specific reputation (1 rep per $10 USD), aligning economic support with governance influence.

## 4. Security & Sybil Resistance
Since the system relies on individual human participation, Sybil resistance is paramount:
- **Hierarchical PoH**: The `IdentityManager` manages a hierarchy of Proof-of-Humanity methods (ZKP > Endorsement > External > Mock).
- **ZKP Integration**: Uses the Semaphore protocol for privacy-preserving identity verification.
- **Security Engine**: Implements graph-based heuristics to detect "Sybil Sinks"—accounts that receive suspicious amounts of delegation from low-activity, low-reputation nodes.

## 5. Fiscal & Verification Loop
A robust "Check and Balance" system for fund release:
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Jurors for milestone verification are randomly selected from a pool, but the algorithm prioritizes those with the highest reputation in the proposal's subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Rep >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, which freezes the treasury payout and slashes the proposer's reputation, creating a strong incentive for honest work.

## 6. Development & Deployment Patterns
- **Script Parity**: Maintaining Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations ensures developer accessibility across platforms.
- **Documentation Standards**: The project mandates a strict set of documentation (`VISION.md`, `IDEAS.md`, `DEPLOY.md`, etc.) which serves as the "source of truth" for both humans and AI agents.
- **PWA Strategy**: The transition to PWA ensures that the "Voluntary Government" is accessible as a mobile app, critical for real-time citizen engagement.

## 7. Versioning & Change Management
- **Single Source of Truth**: The version is sourced from `VERSION.md` and propagated to `package.json` and `CHANGELOG.md` automatically by the protocol.
- **Commit-Push Discipline**: Every major feature or version bump is automatically committed and pushed, maintaining a high-fidelity audit trail of project evolution.

---
*This memory represents the foundational logic of LiquidGov v1.0.37+, establishing a baseline for all future autonomous project execution.*

[PROJECT_MEMORY]

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architectural Summary & Design Patterns

## 1. System Philosophy: The Autonomous Voluntary State
LiquidGov is an experimental governance protocol designed to eliminate bureaucracy through automation. Its core mission is to create a "Voluntary State" where taxation is optional and targeted, and governance is driven by subject-specific expertise rather than raw wealth or simple popularity.

## 2. Technical Stack & Monorepo Structure
The project is a TypeScript-native monorepo that prioritizes simplicity and persistence.
- **Backend**: Express.js REST API with Socket.io for real-time governance events.
- **Frontend**: Vite-based React application using Tailwind CSS v4 and Lucide icons.
- **Database**: SQLite (`better-sqlite3`), chosen for its zero-config persistence and high performance in embedded environments.
- **Monorepo Pattern**: Source code is in `src/`, with `frontend/` as a submodule-like directory. The build process mirrors the source tree into `dist/`, which is critical for Docker and CI/CD deployment paths.

## 3. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The most advanced pattern in LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-governing entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: Automatically reconciles feature branches and `main` to prevent drift.
- **Metadata Governance**: Automatically generates `TODO.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` by scanning the codebase for patterns.
- **Self-Healing Versioning**: Sourced from `VERSION.md`, the protocol ensures consistency across `package.json` and deployment tags.
- **Watchdog Service**: A background task (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) that triggers synchronization cycles every 5 minutes.

## 4. Governance & Economic Patterns
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Power is not global; it is tied to committees (subjects). Users delegate power to experts within specific domains.
- **The "Quadratic" Suite**:
    - **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by making additional votes exponentially more expensive.
    - **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects with a high count of unique contributors over those with single large donors.
- **Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens route capital to specific "subject pools" in the treasury. The `CrowdfundingEngine` pulls matching funds from these specific pools first, ensuring local capital supports local needs.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly increases a citizen's governance influence in that specific subject.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Zero-Knowledge Proofs (ZKP)**: Uses the Semaphore protocol for privacy-preserving human verification.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone release is governed by randomly assigned juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes count double in milestone verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable "Add to Home Screen" and offline access, supporting the goal of broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components (ActionPanel, TreasuryDashboard) use stacking layouts and large touch targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: The `TriageAgent` ranks active proposals for users based on their reputation and partial subject matches (e.g., "Infrastructure" rep boosts "Roads" proposals), surfaced in the `IdentityView`.

## 7. Deployment & CI/CD Patterns
- **Docker Integration**: Uses a multi-stage-ready `Dockerfile` that executes `npx tsc` for the backend and manages the `dist/src/` pathing required by the mirrored directory structure.
- **Script Parity**: Every core shell script (`.sh`) has a corresponding Windows batch file (`.bat`) to ensure the protocol is environment-agnostic.
- **Persistence Patterns**: SQLite tables for `matching_pools` and `treasury_transactions` include donor attribution and subject context for full fiscal auditing.

---
*This memory represents the state of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the foundational context for the continued evolution of the voluntary governance protocol.*

[PROJECT_MEMORY]

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Decisions

## Core Architecture
LiquidGov is a **TypeScript-native monorepo** implementing an autonomous governance protocol. It is composed of a Node.js Express backend, a Vite-React frontend, and an integrated SQLite database. The system is designed around the **"Autonomous Project Execution Protocol" (APEP)**, which treats the codebase as a self-regulating environment.

## Design Patterns & Decisions

### 1. The Autonomous Engine (APEP)
- **Pattern**: Self-governing repository maintenance.
- **Decision**: All repository tasks (merging, documentation, versioning) are handled by internal engines (`src/core/protocol/`).
- **Impact**: Reduces human overhead and ensures that documentation like `ROADMAP.md` and `TODO.md` is always a true reflection of the source code.

### 2. Liquid Governance & Quadratic Economics
- **Pattern**: Multi-dimensional power.
- **Decision**: Voting power is **subject-specific** and **delegatable**.
- **Quadratic Suite**: Implements **Quadratic Voting (QV)** for decision-making and **Quadratic Funding (QF)** for resource allocation to ensure fair representation and community alignment.

### 3. The Voluntary State (Phase 8 Implementation)
- **Decision**: Targeted Voluntary Taxation. Citizens can choose which subject pools (e.g., "Education", "Infrastructure") they fund.
- **Decision**: Stake-based Reputation. Financial contributions are rewarded with subject-specific reputation ($10 = 1 Rep), linking economic support to governance voice.
- **Decision**: Emergency Overrides. Critical issues can bypass standard sponsorship to reach immediate voting.

### 4. Verification & Accountability
- **Pattern**: Randomized Expert Juries.
- **Decision**: Milestone fund release requires jury approval, with experts (Rep >= 10) receiving weighted votes (2x power).
- **Decision**: Slashing & Disputes. Juries can reject milestones, halting funds and penalizing the proposer's reputation, creating a high-trust verification loop.

### 5. Identity & Security
- **Pattern**: Hierarchical Proof-of-Humanity.
- **Decision**: Prioritize **Zero-Knowledge Proofs (Semaphore)** for privacy, followed by endorsements and external verification.
- **Decision**: Sybil Detection. The `SecurityEngine` uses graph analysis to identify and flag suspicious delegation sinks during governance transitions.

### 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **Pattern**: Mobile-First PWA.
- **Decision**: Use `vite-plugin-pwa` and responsive Tailwind layouts to ensure the protocol is accessible as a mobile app, critical for real-time citizen participation in emergency votes.
- **Decision**: Intelligent Discovery. A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces relevant proposals based on a user's reputation profile.

## Infrastructure & Environment
- **Database**: SQLite (`better-sqlite3`) for persistent, portable state.
- **CI/CD**: Mirroring source structure into `dist/` to maintain absolute path consistency for Docker and automation scripts.
- **Script Symmetry**: Unix/Windows parity (`.sh` and `.bat`) for all protocol operations.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical baseline of LiquidGov v1.0.53.*

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architectural & Pattern Analysis

LiquidGov is an autonomous governance monorepo designed to facilitate a "Voluntary State" through a decentralized, expert-led, and economically incentivized framework. Below is a summary of the core architecture, established patterns, and strategic design decisions.

## 1. Core Monorepo Architecture
LiquidGov follows a **TypeScript-native Monorepo** structure.
- **Backend**: Express.js REST API with Socket.io for real-time state updates.
- **Frontend**: Vite-powered React application with Tailwind CSS v4.
- **Persistence**: SQLite via `better-sqlite3`. The decision to use SQLite ensures zero-configuration persistence, high performance for complex recursive queries (like delegation power), and ease of environment portability.
- **Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler mirrors the `src/` directory into `dist/`. This structure is critical for maintainability, but requires careful handling of relative paths in Docker and CI/CD pipelines (e.g., the entrypoint is `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The Autonomous Project Execution Protocol (APEP)
The most unique pattern in this project is the **Executive Protocol**. The codebase is treated as a self-governing entity that manages its own lifecycle via engines in `src/core/protocol/`:
- **Dual-Direction Intelligent Merge**: An automated engine that forward-merges feature branches into `main` and reverse-merges `main` back into features to prevent drift.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for specific patterns (TODO tags, version bumps).
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository remains in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The project rejects simple majority-rule in favor of **Quadratic Economics**:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Ensures that intensity of preference matters. Users can cast multiple votes for a single proposal, but the cost (in Voice Credits) increases quadratically ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large number of individual citizens rather than a few large donors. The matching formula $(\sum \sqrt{c_i})^2 - \sum c_i$ is implemented in the `TreasuryManager`.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is not global. It is tied to subjects (committees). Users can delegate their power to experts within specific domains, creating a "Liquid Democracy" where expertise flows to the point of decision.

## 4. The Voluntary State Infrastructure (v1.0.53+)
Strategic decisions made to implement the "Voluntary State" mission:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (Education, Infrastructure, etc.) they fund. The `CrowdfundingEngine` implements intelligent routing, pulling matching funds from subject-specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy pattern where financial support rewards the citizen with subject-specific reputation ($10 USD = 1 Rep), directly linking financial sustainability to governance influence.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status allowing critical proposals to bypass sponsorship and reach immediate voting, ensuring the system can respond to urgent infrastructure or safety issues.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack where ZKP (Semaphore) is the strongest method, followed by Peer Endorsement and External verification.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes count double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Slashing Mechanism**: A dispute system allows juries to reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation.

## 6. Frontend & Deployment Patterns
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` for offline access and mobile installation.
- **Mobile-First UX**: Responsive Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets to accommodate the "Voluntary Government" on any device.
- **Script Symmetry**: Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry is maintained for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`) to ensure the protocol is platform-agnostic.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Strategic Decisions

LiquidGov is a decentralized, autonomous governance protocol designed to facilitate a "Voluntary State." The system prioritizes expert-led decision-making, economic accountability, and automated repository management.

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is built as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, integrating the full stack into a single, cohesive codebase.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events and state synchronization.
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite-powered application using React, Tailwind CSS v4, and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) is used for all state persistence. The choice of SQLite provides a high-performance, zero-configuration database that is portable and suitable for embedded or autonomous environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler is configured to mirror the `src/` structure into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency between development and production, though it requires specific path handling in Docker and CI/CD (e.g., the server entry point is `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) that automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation such as `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns and tags.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) integrated into the API server that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository is always in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large count of unique contributors over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Power is not global; it is tied to committees/subjects. Citizens delegate power to subject-matter experts, creating a domain-specific "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Infrastructure (v1.0.53+)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly increases a citizen's governance influence in that specific subject.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and go straight to active voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Peer Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes count double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

## 7. Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry is maintained for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Strict Versioning**: The project follows a single-source-of-truth versioning strategy via `VERSION.md`, which is propagated to `package.json` and the `CHANGELOG.md` by the autonomous protocol.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events and state synchronization.
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite-powered application using React, Tailwind CSS v4, and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) is used for all state persistence. The choice of SQLite provides a high-performance, zero-configuration database that is portable and suitable for embedded or autonomous environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler is configured to mirror the `src/` structure into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency between development and production, though it requires specific path handling in Docker and CI/CD (e.g., the server entry point is `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) that automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation such as `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns and tags.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) integrated into the API server that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository is always in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large count of unique contributors over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Power is not global; it is tied to committees/subjects. Citizens delegate power to subject-matter experts, creating a domain-specific "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Infrastructure (v1.0.53+)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly increases a citizen's governance influence in that specific subject.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and go straight to active voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Peer Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes count double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

## 7. Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry is maintained for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Strict Versioning**: The project follows a single-source-of-truth versioning strategy via `VERSION.md`, which is propagated to `package.json` and the `CHANGELOG.md` by the autonomous protocol.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architecture & Strategic Design Baseline (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is an autonomous governance protocol built as a self-healing monorepo. Its mission is to facilitate a "Voluntary State" where taxation is targeted, expertise is prioritized, and repository management is fully automated.

## 1. Core Technical Architecture
- **TypeScript Monorepo**: A unified codebase containing both the Express.js backend and Vite-React frontend.
- **Persistence Layer**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-config data storage. It is used to persist complex governance structures including recursive delegations, multi-token matching pools, and granular transaction ledgers.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The system is designed to mirror the `src/` directory structure into `dist/` during compilation. This ensures absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts, Docker containers, and CI/CD pipelines.

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
A defining architectural pattern of the project is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This internal engine handles the repository's lifecycle:
- **Intelligent Merging**: Automatically reconciles feature branches and `main` using conflict-aware strategies.
- **Dynamic Documentation**: Mandatory files like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` are automatically generated or updated via codebase scanning during every protocol cycle.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) ensures continuous synchronization and deployment readiness.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The protocol utilizes **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair representation and community alignment:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

## 4. The Voluntary State Loop (Phase 8 Implementation)
The project has established a circular economy for the "Voluntary State":
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A direct linkage between economic support and governance influence ($10 USD = 1 subject-specific reputation point).
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety proposals, allowing them to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate citizen voting.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **Mobile-First PWA**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, ensuring the voluntary state is accessible on any device.
- **Intelligent Discovery**: A triage-based suggestion engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and historical participation patterns.

## 7. Deployment & Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Complete Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Environment Consistency**: Docker configurations use `dist/src/api/server.js` as the entrypoint to accommodate the mirrored source structure.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical foundation of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is built as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, integrating the full stack into a single, cohesive codebase.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events and state synchronization.
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite-powered application using React, Tailwind CSS v4, and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) is used for all state persistence. The choice of SQLite provides a high-performance, zero-configuration database that is portable and suitable for embedded or autonomous environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler is configured to mirror the `src/` structure into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency between development and production, though it requires specific path handling in Docker and CI/CD (e.g., the server entry point is `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)

- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) that automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).

- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

## 4. The Voluntary State Loop (Phase 8 Implementation)
The project has established a circular economy for the "Voluntary State":
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A direct linkage between economic support and governance influence ($10 USD = 1 subject-specific reputation point).
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety proposals, allowing them to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate citizen voting.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **Mobile-First PWA**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, ensuring the voluntary state is accessible on any device.
- **Intelligent Discovery**: A triage-based suggestion engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and historical participation patterns.

## 7. Deployment & Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Complete Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Authorization**: Sensitive governance actions (voting, depositing, discovery) are secured via JWT middleware, enforcing that the `userId` in the token matches the action's subject.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical foundation of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Strategic Decisions

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is designed as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, ensuring full-stack type safety and structural consistency.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API using Socket.io for real-time state synchronization.
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite + React application using Tailwind CSS v4 for styling.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-configuration data storage. This decision ensures portability and reliability for autonomous or embedded governance environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The system mirrors the `src/` directory into `dist/` during compilation. This allows for absolute path consistency in Docker and CI/CD pipelines, with the server entry point stabilized at `dist/src/api/server.js`.

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of the project is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol (APEP)**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) automatically reconciles feature branches and `main` using conflict-aware strategies.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated via codebase scanning for specific patterns and tags.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service triggers synchronization and verification cycles every 5 minutes, ensuring the repository is always in a deployable state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair representation and community alignment:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with single large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is not global; it is tied to domain-specific committees. Citizens delegate their power to experts, creating an expertise-driven "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Implementation (v1.0.53+)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund via targeted matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly increases a citizen's governance influence in that specific subject.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone release verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and mobile installation, essential for broad citizen participation in emergency votes.
- **Mobile-First UX**: Responsive Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation system surfaces relevant proposals based on a user's reputation profile and expertise.

## 7. Deployment Standards
- **Script Parity**: Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry is maintained for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`) to ensure the protocol is environment-agnostic.
- **Strict Versioning**: A single-source-of-truth strategy via `VERSION.md`, which is propagated to `package.json` and `CHANGELOG.md` by the autonomous protocol.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical baseline of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The protocol utilizes **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair representation and community alignment:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.

- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety proposals, allowing them to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate citizen voting.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Strategic Decisions

LiquidGov is a decentralized, autonomous governance protocol designed to facilitate a "Voluntary State." The system prioritizes expert-led decision-making, economic accountability, and automated repository management.

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is built as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, ensuring full-stack type safety and structural consistency.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events and state synchronization.
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite-powered application using React, Tailwind CSS v4, and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) is used for all state persistence. The choice of SQLite provides a high-performance, zero-configuration database that is portable and suitable for embedded or autonomous environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler is configured to mirror the `src/` structure into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency between development and production, though it requires specific path handling in Docker and CI/CD (e.g., the server entry point is `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) that automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation such as `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns and tags.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) integrated into the API server that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository is always in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large count of unique contributors over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Power is not global; it is tied to committees/subjects. Citizens delegate power to subject-matter experts, creating a domain-specific "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Infrastructure (v1.0.53+)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly increases a citizen's governance influence in that specific subject.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and go straight to active voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Peer Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes count double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

## 7. Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry is maintained for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Strict Versioning**: The project follows a single-source-of-truth versioning strategy via `VERSION.md`, which is propagated to `package.json` and the `CHANGELOG.md` by the autonomous protocol.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Implementation (Phase 8)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted treasury matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly rewards the citizen with subject-specific reputation, increasing their governance influence in that domain.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Strategic Decisions (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is a decentralized, autonomous governance protocol designed to facilitate a **"Voluntary State."** The system prioritizes domain-specific expertise, economic accountability, and high-fidelity automation.

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is structured as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, ensuring full-stack type safety and structural consistency.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events (e.g., `TREASURY_UPDATED`, `PROPOSAL_UPDATED`).
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite + React application using Tailwind CSS v4 and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-configuration persistence. This decision ensures portability and reliability in autonomous or embedded environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler mirrors the `src/` directory into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts and CI/CD pipelines, with the server entry point stabilized at `dist/src/api/server.js`.

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation such as `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns and tags during every protocol cycle.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) integrated into the API server that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository is always in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large count of unique contributors over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is not global; it is tied to domain-specific committees. Citizens delegate power to experts, creating an expertise-driven "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Implementation (Phase 8)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted treasury matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly rewards the citizen with subject-specific reputation, increasing their governance influence in that domain.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Peer Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation in emergency votes.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

---
*This baseline establishes the foundation for LiquidGov's evolution into a fully autonomous, voluntary, and expert-driven governance protocol.*

[PROJECT_MEMORY]

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architecture & Strategic Design Baseline (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is an autonomous governance protocol built as a self-healing monorepo. Its mission is to facilitate a "Voluntary State" where taxation is targeted, expertise is prioritized, and repository management is fully automated.

## 1. Core Technical Architecture
- **TypeScript Monorepo**: A unified codebase containing both the Express.js backend and Vite-React frontend. This ensures type safety across the network boundary and simplifies dependency management.
- **Persistence Layer**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-config data storage. It is used to persist complex governance structures including recursive delegations, multi-token matching pools, and granular transaction ledgers.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The system is designed to mirror the `src/` directory structure into `dist/` during compilation. This ensures absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts, Docker containers, and CI/CD pipelines, though it requires specific handling of entry points (e.g., `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
A defining architectural pattern of the project is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This internal engine handles the repository's lifecycle:
- **Intelligent Merging**: Automatically reconciles feature branches and `main` using conflict-aware strategies (like `ours` for structural preservation) to prevent environment drift.
- **Dynamic Documentation**: Mandatory files like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` are automatically generated or updated via codebase scanning during every protocol cycle.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) ensures continuous synchronization and deployment readiness by triggering the protocol every 5 minutes.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The protocol utilizes **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair representation and community alignment:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

## 4. The Voluntary State Loop (Phase 8 Implementation)
The project has established a circular economy for the "Voluntary State":
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A direct linkage between economic support and governance influence ($10 USD = 1 subject-specific reputation point).
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety proposals, allowing them to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate citizen voting.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **Mobile-First PWA**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, ensuring the voluntary state is accessible on any device.
- **Intelligent Discovery**: A triage-based suggestion engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and historical participation patterns.

## 7. Deployment & Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Complete Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Authorization**: Sensitive governance actions (voting, depositing, discovery) are secured via JWT middleware, enforcing that the `userId` in the token matches the action's subject.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical foundation of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).

- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.

- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.

- **Intelligent Discovery**: A triage-based suggestion engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and historical participation patterns.

## 7. Deployment & Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Complete Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Authorization**: Sensitive governance actions (voting, depositing, discovery) are secured via JWT middleware, enforcing that the `userId` in the token matches the action's subject.

- **Authorization Hardening**: Sensitive governance actions (Discovery, Jury Voting, Deposits) are secured via JWT middleware, preventing impersonation.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation in emergency votes.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile, with scores boosted by partial subject matches.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical baseline of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

# LiquidGov: Architecture, Patterns, and Strategic Decisions (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is a decentralized, autonomous governance protocol designed to facilitate a **"Voluntary State."** The system prioritize domain-specific expertise, economic accountability, and high-fidelity automation.

## 1. Core Architecture: The Monolithic Monorepo
LiquidGov is structured as a **TypeScript-native monorepo**, ensuring full-stack type safety and structural consistency.
- **Backend**: An Express.js REST API utilizing Socket.io for real-time governance events (e.g., `TREASURY_UPDATED`, `PROPOSAL_UPDATED`).
- **Frontend**: A reactive Vite + React application using Tailwind CSS v4 and Lucide icons.
- **Persistence**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-configuration persistence. This decision ensures portability and reliability in autonomous or embedded environments.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The TypeScript compiler mirrors the `src/` directory structure into `dist/`. This ensures absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts and CI/CD pipelines, with the server entry point stabilized at `dist/src/api/server.js`.

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity:
- **Intelligent Merging**: An internal engine (`src/core/protocol/`) automatically reconciles AI-generated feature branches with `main`, using conflict-aware strategies like `ours` to preserve advanced infrastructure.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation such as `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is not manually maintained; it is automatically generated and synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns and tags during every protocol cycle.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) integrated into the API server that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository is always in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
LiquidGov implements **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by making the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal increase exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that prioritizes projects supported by a large count of unique contributors over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees, creating an expertise-driven "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The "Voluntary State" Implementation (Phase 8)
Strategic decisions made to implement a fair and minimal fiscal loop:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund via targeted treasury matching pools.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly rewards the citizen with subject-specific reputation, increasing their governance influence in that domain.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate voting via specialized API logic.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Peer Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility Decisions
- **PWA Strategy**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, essential for broad citizen participation.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets (44px+) for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical baseline of LiquidGov v1.0.53.*

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architectural & Strategic Design Baseline (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is a decentralized, autonomous governance protocol built as a self-regulating monorepo. It implements a "Voluntary State" architecture where bureaucracy is replaced by automation, taxation is optional and targeted, and power is earned through domain expertise and material support.

## 1. Core Technical Architecture
- **Monolithic Monorepo**: A TypeScript-native monorepo that ensures full-stack type safety. The backend and frontend share models, ensuring that governance logic remains consistent across the network boundary.
- **REST & Real-Time API**: An Express.js backend provides a robust RESTful interface for governance actions, complemented by Socket.io for real-time state synchronization (e.g., live treasury updates, proposal status changes).
- **Persistent State (SQLite)**: Utilizing `better-sqlite3` for high-performance, zero-configuration persistence. SQLite was chosen to ensure the protocol is portable, easy to deploy in restricted environments, and capable of handling recursive delegation queries with low latency.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The system is designed to mirror the `src/` directory structure into `dist/` during compilation. This maintains absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts and Docker containers, with the server entry point stabilized at `dist/src/api/server.js`.

## 2. The Autonomous Project Execution Protocol (APEP)
The defining feature of LiquidGov is its ability to govern its own development. This system treats the codebase as a self-regulating entity via engines in `src/core/protocol/`:
- **Dual-Direction Intelligent Merge**: An automated engine that forward-merges AI-generated feature branches into `main` and reverse-merges `main` back into features, ensuring environment parity and preventing drift.
- **Metadata Governance**: Documentation like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` is automatically synchronized by scanning the codebase for patterns, version bumps, and task tags.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) that periodically triggers the protocol to ensure the repository remains in a "ready-to-deploy" state.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The protocol rejects simple majority-rule in favor of **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair and intense preference representation:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates "tyranny of the majority" by scaling the cost of multiple votes for a single proposal exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors, utilizing the treasury matching pools.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is not global; it is tied to committees (subjects). Citizens delegate power to subject-matter experts, creating a fluid, domain-specific "Liquid Democracy."

## 4. The Voluntary State Infrastructure
LiquidGov implements a fair and minimal fiscal loop through several key strategic decisions:
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which committees (e.g., Education, Infrastructure) they fund. The `CrowdfundingEngine` implements intelligent routing, sourcing matching capital from these specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A circular economy where financial support ($10 USD = 1 Rep) directly rewards the citizen with subject-specific reputation, increasing their governance influence.
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety issues, allowing proposals to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate active voting.

## 5. Security, Sybil Resistance & Accountability
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`. ZKP is treated as the gold standard for privacy-preserving verification.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone releasing verifications.
- **Milestone Disputes & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestone proofs, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes funds and slashes the proposer’s reputation.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **PWA Strategy**: Integrated `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and mobile installation, crucial for broad citizen engagement in emergency response scenarios.
- **Mobile-Responsive UI**: Refined Tailwind components (ActionPanel, TreasuryDashboard) utilize stacking layouts and touch-optimized targets for high-fidelity mobile interaction.
- **Discovery Engine**: A triage-based recommendation system surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and reputation profile.

---
*This baseline documents the technical and philosophical state of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*

# LiquidGov: Comprehensive Architecture & Strategic Design Baseline (v1.0.53)

LiquidGov is an autonomous governance protocol built as a self-healing monorepo. Its mission is to facilitate a "Voluntary State" where taxation is targeted, expertise is prioritized, and repository management is fully automated.

## 1. Core Technical Architecture
- **TypeScript Monorepo**: A unified codebase containing both the Express.js backend and Vite-React frontend. This ensures type safety across the network boundary and simplifies dependency management.
- **Persistence Layer**: **SQLite** (via `better-sqlite3`) provides high-performance, zero-config data storage. It is used to persist complex governance structures including recursive delegations, multi-token matching pools, and granular transaction ledgers.
- **Mirrored Build Pattern**: The system is designed to mirror the `src/` directory structure into `dist/` during compilation. This ensures absolute path consistency for autonomous scripts, Docker containers, and CI/CD pipelines, though it requires specific handling of entry points (e.g., `dist/src/api/server.js`).

## 2. The "Executive Protocol" (APEP)
A defining architectural pattern of the project is the **Autonomous Project Execution Protocol**. This internal engine handles the repository's lifecycle:
- **Intelligent Merging**: Automatically reconciles feature branches and `main` using conflict-aware strategies (like `ours` for structural preservation) to prevent environment drift.
- **Dynamic Documentation**: Mandatory files like `ROADMAP.md`, `TODO.md`, and `CHANGELOG.md` are automatically generated or updated via codebase scanning during every protocol cycle.
- **Autonomous Watchdog**: A background service (`src/core/watchdog.ts`) ensures continuous synchronization and deployment readiness by triggering the protocol every 5 minutes.

## 3. Governance & Economic Design (The Quadratic Suite)
The protocol utilizes **Quadratic Economics** to ensure fair representation and community alignment:
- **Quadratic Voting (QV)**: Mitigates majority dominance by scaling the cost of multiple votes exponentially ($Cost = Votes^2$).
- **Quadratic Funding (QF)**: A matching engine that amplifies projects supported by a large number of unique individual citizens over those with a few large donors.
- **Subject-Specific Liquid Power**: Voting power is domain-specific. Citizens delegate their power to experts within specific committees (subjects), creating a fluid, expertise-driven democracy.

## 4. The Voluntary State Loop (Phase 8 Implementation)
The project has established a circular economy for the "Voluntary State":
- **Targeted Voluntary Taxation**: Citizens choose which subject pools (e.g., Infrastructure, Education) they fund. The matching engine intelligently routes capital from these specific pools first.
- **Stake-based Reputation**: A direct linkage between economic support and governance influence ($10 USD = 1 subject-specific reputation point).
- **Emergency Overrides**: A fast-track status for critical infrastructure or safety proposals, allowing them to bypass standard sponsorship and reach immediate citizen voting.

## 5. Security & Verification Infrastructure
- **Identity Hierarchy**: Manages Proof-of-Humanity through a prioritized stack: `ZKP (Semaphore) > Endorsement > External > Mock`.
- **Expert-Prioritized Juries**: Milestone verifications are conducted by juries that prioritize citizens with the highest reputation in the relevant subject area.
- **Weighted Quorum**: Experts (Reputation >= 10) have their votes weighted double in milestone verifications.
- **Dispute & Slashing**: Juries can reject milestones, triggering an `isDisputed` state that freezes treasury payouts and slashes the proposer’s reputation by 10 points.

## 6. Frontend & Accessibility
- **Mobile-First PWA**: Configured via `vite-plugin-pwa` to enable offline access and "Add to Home Screen" functionality, ensuring the voluntary state is accessible on any device.
- **Intelligent Discovery**: A triage-based suggestion engine surfaces proposals to users based on their established expertise and historical participation patterns.

## 7. Deployment & Operational Standards
- **Script Parity**: Complete Unix (`.sh`) and Windows (`.bat`) symmetry for all core operations (`build`, `start`, `sync-protocol`).
- **Authorization**: Sensitive governance actions (voting, depositing, discovery) are secured via JWT middleware, enforcing that the `userId` in the token matches the action's subject.

---
*This summary documents the technical and philosophical foundation of LiquidGov v1.0.53, serving as the definitive context for future autonomous execution.*
